**Current follow-through — native support criteria independently reviewed; runtime applicability reviewed; remaining support branch assigned.**

The one-tight native family now has two further conditional results. When both P columns contain their free-group tight row, the admitted four-vertex scalar full-slot bound and probability normalization exclude every upper endpoint. With r=n_T/n_F<1 and s=min(t)/max(t), the normalized endpoint bound is 1-19s-14(1-r)(1-s). The admitted lower bound gives s>1/19. The two pure-axis candidates at the zero endpoint are also strictly negative. The complete family's maximum therefore has exactly the sign of the common-demand diagonal score. Its sharp necessary condition at the original lex-selected optimum is sum(n_g*d_g)>28*sum(n_g)-36*n_F; primary optimality alone gives the weak inequality. A negative numerical maximum need not equal the diagonal score.

A separately reviewed corollary covers one free-supported column with the other column empty. The family admits a finite strict primary improvement exactly when the empty-axis reward is positive or t_empty/t_free>3. After excluding a positive empty-axis reward, equality at3 gives zero primary gain and original lex descent; a ratio below3 makes the complete normalized family strictly negative. Neutral rays do not automatically give zero total gain. Both results preserve the actual same-rest support premises, native minimum payments, complete finite-step margins and original full-vector lex order.

# Current reviewed progress

The both-tight-mu specialization is accepted after root and independent manual review. Equal tight mu values force every present P column bound to have the same cap Y=a0/max(n0,n1), including equal-coefficient and zero-nu ties. With both caps absent, finite equal demands improve the primary objective. With one missing cap, improvement exists exactly when that column's pure-axis reward is positive; a zero axial reward leaves a neutral ray attached to a strictly negative total score.

The proposed blanket exclusion when both caps are present required correction. With A0=36*p*a0/960, R=14*p*(n0+n1)/960 and r=min(n0,n1)/max(n0,n1), the proposed bound -A0+2RY equals p*a0*(28r-8)/960. It is negative only for r<2/7, which has not been established at the actual law. The exact maximum is the largest of the diagonal score B=-A0+SY and the two bounded axis scores. Both axis scores are strictly negative, so the maximum's sign is exactly B's. The necessary lex-optimal diagonal-price inequality is strict; equality gives an original full-vector lex descent. A failed upper-bound shortcut is not a realizable native counterexample.

These remain conditional results for the assigned sign family. Actual support, coefficient values, the opposite-sector comparison, a full dual and a normalized witness remain unresolved. All original equations, recovery, finite-step margins, strict restoration, endpoints and labels remain. The next native proof question, RI353, specializes the one-tight-mu branch using its same-law support relations and the accepted coefficient ordering, testing whether the bounded candidate list reduces to endpoints.

The documentation, pair-kernel, certificate and research-runner implementers
have completed their handoffs and released their source reservations. The
coordinator accepted those bounded changes. RI-11 has also completed review
and released its source reservations. RI-12's exact contract and synthetic
public-API comparator fixture, the eighteenth registry integration, RI-13's
ledger, RI-14's metadata and RI-15's exact adapter are complete; their implementation reservations
are released.
Reserve any source needed for the next
independent application-contract task before editing it. Completed QR
bundles remain byte-stable; successor proofs use new isolated sources.

These reservations prevent overlapping edits in the shared checkout; they are
not permanent ownership of the project. Preserve pre-existing changes. Owners
must request a shared file before editing it and release their reservation in
the completion handoff. The coordinator records acceptance, rather than
assuming that an idle task or a green test count closes a work item. Existing
captured research results remain historical evidence; repairs use new source
or explicit successor notes instead of rewriting old outcomes.

**Remote checkpoints:** the user subsequently explicitly requested commits
and pushes during work updates. The coordinator owns git/index operations
for this program, currently on `ret` with upstream `origin/ret`. After a
source-stable bounded result passes review, stage its exact scope and required
dependencies, validate that checkpoint, commit and push it, and record the
verified remote commit. Preserve unrelated pre-existing changes and ongoing
QR source work. The earlier no-commit/no-push statements describe historical
cycles; they are superseded for future scoped checkpoints. QR has acknowledged
central coordination of git operations.

The [publication backlog](docs/coordination/PUBLICATION_BACKLOG.md) distinguishes
accepted local work from complete remote checkpoints. RI-02/RI-04 repairs and
RI-12 exact identifiability are published in remotely verified `OBSCURED-ID-00704`:
203 focused tests and 90 legacy research checks pass in its isolated source
candidate without the unpublished core/RET baseline. RI-11 is published in remotely verified `OBSCURED-ID-00705`, with 105 focused tests
and 33 legacy checks passing in its isolated chronology/consumer candidate.
The comparator's RET imports and RI-14 support/claim prerequisites remain separate publication work.

A mathematical obstruction completes a particular rejected candidate, not the
whole foundations program. Do not repeatedly try to prove a statement already
refuted under the unchanged premises. Use the failure to identify the next
explicit premise or smaller useful theorem. Conversely, do not treat additional
axioms authorized for investigation as having been physically derived or
automatically adopted globally.

**RI-19 bounded interface-robustness design assignment — completed and accepted**

This completed assignment followed RI-18's verified publication and closes a concrete
compatibility gap between approximate local operations and the exact joint
interface. Small repeatability error or raw distance alone need not put an
output in C0: arbitrarily near-ideal positive full-C outputs can retain
nonzero c and fail the polarized-reference recordability requirement. Adding
more measurements after the ideal Y reset would not repair either issue.

Scope this first as a proof/design note, with an independent review before
any executor or witness implementation. QR may edit exactly the new
`docs/coordination/QR_INTERFACE_ROBUSTNESS_PLAN.md` and its three existing
QR maps/handoff. Root owns this plan, progress/publication records, all
source-pinned integration and git/index. Preserve the 60 previously held
predecessor/design paths, the five accepted RI-18 source/statement files and
the registry. No earlier theorem, source, launcher or design is repinned.

The note must make the following targets concrete and either prove them or
state the precise remaining obstruction:

1. Classify positive real-linear maps C→C0 with one exactly calibrated
   rank-one effect p=e_Q(d), and both complementary branches' complete
   calibration. With c=u+iv, test the candidate form
   B̃_Q(d)=J2(pH+uK+vL), with Hermitian H,K,L, Tr H=1 and
   Tr K=Tr L=0. Prove the projected input domain p≥2|c| and necessity
   and sufficiency of H+(cos θ K+sin θ L)/2≥0 for every θ. Derive
   the corresponding uniform repeatability condition for 0≤ε≤1/2;
   a sampled angle grid or assumed quotient descent is not a classification.
   Identify the Φ-descending subclass (candidate K=L=0) and the ε=0
   ideal-reset limit; exact C0 output alone does not imply either property.
2. Exhibit a positive near-ideal full-C output with nonzero c that fails
   the existing polarized-reference interface when a ready branch has
   positive weight. Establish the contrasting lawful C→C0 class and a
   nonfactorizing member if available. No J2∘Φ projection, recoupling,
   unannounced preparation or widened return cone repairs a failed path.
3. Specify the hypothetical approximate first instrument, its availability
   and complete typed prefix/record/weight/premise contract. The accepted
   RI-18 WeightedLocal derives the ideal branch; a different output cannot
   be inserted into that executor or certified by forging an ideal record.
   A mathematically compatible output and an executable typed interchange
   are separate obligations. Identify actual unchanged suffix interfaces
   and any new adapter obligation, without implementing it in this stage.
4. Propagate the branch error through the fixed RI-18 finite suffix with
   the same independent reference law and readiness β. Establish or
   correct the candidate normalized scalar-law bound TV≤β√ε and
   the same-initial-Y comparison bound min(1,2β√ε), with any branch-specific
   bound and homogeneous incoming-mass extension stated separately. Contract from
   the C0 base/raw-trace norm into the complete scalar law; do not assume
   raw-trace contraction in the native L_t representation. Include
   zero-selected-event refusals, all zero-weight coordinates and β=0,1.
5. Define an explicit common scalar comparison alphabet retaining every
   prospective outcome, setting, selector/preparation ID, command and
   terminal/stop label. Different instrument/premise audit types are not
   equal wrapper objects. Changed observed setting labels cannot silently
   be identified to claim small TV. Show which initial coordinates the
   fixed read catalogue actually observes: rank≤4 is a target bound,
   not evidence that any c-dependence survives the suffix. Distinguish
   exact rank, quantitative distinguishability and finite-sample precision.

Whole-C positivity, exactly calibrated effects, exact C0 range, supplied
branch/preparation/read availability, uniform error and fixed comparison
metadata remain explicit premises. At β=0 only the two calibrated stops
are observed, regardless of differing retained audit sources. Do not claim
sharp composed constants, physical error calibration, unknown-instrument
identification, a full quantum reconstruction or measured advantage without
the corresponding proof/data. No generic positivity solver, process SDK,
RET/bank work, new control catalogue or geometry work is assigned.

Return a source-quiet design/proof handoff with independent mathematical and
API/record review, exact dependency identities and any supplemental arithmetic
clearly distinguished from theorem proof or pinned execution. Stop for
coordinator acceptance. A subsequent exact implementation requires a separate
assignment; after resolving this interface, consolidate the connected argument
rather than automatically adding another operation family. The next larger
   decision is a premise-selection theorem that excludes admitted countermodels
   or a measured application with a supplied calibration/evaluation contract.

Root dispatched this assignment after independently verifying RI-20 publication

Hermitian D have a negative principal minor D_I of order m. Supply the single
fixed seed B=bb†, b=(1,−1,1), with total-entry mass one. A candidate event uses

use n copies of D and the same one seed, if desired.

both directions of the finite catalogue equivalence. Include every nonempty
principal subset, not only leading minors. Explicitly retain the fixed carrier
size, normalization, identical independent copies, tensor product and full
joint-event positivity. The signed pure seed is a stronger specified resource
than a generic member outside R+; its admission is not derived. Neither
minimality, experimental sharp-read availability nor uniform noisy precision
is claimed. Credit the standard determinant/antisymmetrization identity and
its relation to the existing composition arguments without asserting novelty.

The exact 3-by-3 example [[1,1,1],[1,1,−1],[1,−1,1]]/5 is weak positive,
with all order-two principal minors zero but determinant −4/125; its order-three
event should weigh −24/125. It prevents an erroneous two-minor-only test.

arithmetic, not a new executor, registry integration, predecessor replay or

**Historical RI-22 assignment — now independently accepted**

Let D=R+iJ, with R real symmetric PSD, J real antisymmetric and total-entry
mass one. For E=S+iK define D star E=R tensor S+i(J tensor S+R tensor K).
Prove or correct class closure, all-Boolean weak positivity, normalization,
associativity, permutation covariance, unit, separate affinity, full complex
marginals and scalar rectangle independence. Establish separate real-map
naturality. Partition maps preserve normalization; they are sufficient, not
necessary. For arbitrary real U, audit the universal mass condition
U^T1=±1 rather than treating partition incidence as an iff.

The missing term from ordinary tensor composition is −J tensor K. Show why
full event-pair rectangle multiplicativity, together with biadditivity, fixes
the ordinary tensor entries whereas scalar independence does not. Complex
bilinearity alone is not an exclusion: the candidate has the extension
A star B=A tensor B−A_a tensor B_a, A_a=(A−A^T)/2. Its unnormalized cone
has imaginary lineality, so positive scalar effects annihilate that sector.
State what is formally retained and what the declared scalar catalogue can
observe, without claiming a faithful operational theory.

For the real RI-21 seed, the permutation event under star has weight
m!det(R_I). For Di this is +1/2, versus −3/2 under the ordinary product.
Keep the accepted theorem unchanged: this countermodel alters its tensor-law
premise. It need not preserve arbitrary complex congruences or the accepted
QR instrument classes; hypothetical richer controls or separating effects
remain additional premises. No new global axiom, physical operation or
replacement of an existing composition implementation is proposed.

**Application decision**

The initial application priority is an offline calibration or measurement
decision, with explicit covariance, cost and model inadequacy. Current RET
supports static Gaussian/correlated models and one bounded exponential
response; it does not expose general stochastic drift, mixtures or change
points. Start with a compatible problem or validate the needed extension.

QR's initial contribution should be an answerability/measurement contract:
which observations identify the requested target, what uncertainty remains,
which model assumptions the decoder uses, and what additional measurement
could distinguish unresolved alternatives. Evaluate against ordinary
calibration/design, direct simulation or standard refinement methods. Retain a
history coordinate only if held-out utility supports it. A real-data pilot
requires a suitable source, license, units, timestamps, acquisition context,
conventional baselines and held-out evaluation; absent data is a named
dependency, not permission to call synthetic data measured.

The current QR composition also has a proved application boundary: its fixed
first Y branch makes the complete scalar outcome law depend only on initial
mass and Y expectation. The rank-two span map and exact normalized total
variation identity are recorded in the
[application plan](docs/coordination/APPLICATION_WORK_PLAN.md). Original source
audit anchors are not additional observations. Full accounting in RI-18 does
not provide initial-state tomography; a different pre-reset question requires
its own supplied operation and fresh-source premises.

**Recurring follow-through**

Use an hourly follow-up in this coordination task. At each run:

1. Read this plan, the progress record and QR handoff; inspect the current
   working tree and task statuses before editing or sending a new assignment.
2. Review new QR results when present. If QR is still working, preserve its
   reservations and advance an independent coordinator item. Do not restart,
   duplicate or interrupt an unchanged running assignment.
3. Complete a bounded implementation/proof-review item with appropriate checks.
   Reconcile findings into the progress record and update actual states here.
   At a reviewed source-stable boundary, make a scoped commit and push the
   update to the verified upstream; record its identity and remote result.
4. Send a concrete next QR assignment only when its predecessor is reviewed or
   a new result changes the needed direction. Keep the full record/residual,
   lawful sequencing, precursor and nontermination requirements explicit.
5. Surface decisions requiring missing user information, external data or
   authority with a precise reason. Continue other unblocked work. Do not
   promote a theorem, release, calibration, ontology or physical claim merely
   to close a row.

The cadence is a coordination interval, not an execution-time estimate or a
promise of continuous computation. Local scheduled work requires the computer,
application and checkout to remain available. Existing acceptance thresholds,
protected validation material, physical experiments and publication remain
subject to their actual project-specific prerequisites. No broad staging,
commit, push or destructive cleanup was part of the first cycle. The later
explicit user instruction now requires scoped commits and pushes during
completed work updates, as specified above.

**RI-39 — history measure and a geometry-regime screen, assigned**

Independent review identifies a concrete consequence worth resolving before
larger simulations: RI-38's explicit epsilon/complement choice gives every
newly constructed row full-precursor probability greater than one half. Each
such birth increases longest-chain height by one. This is a feature of that
chosen normalization, not a necessity proved for every admitted extension.

QR now reserves only `docs/track_b/native_growth_history_height_v1/HISTORY_HEIGHT.md`
and an optional useful standalone `check.py` there. With a fixed admissible
finite seed and the explicit recursive selection, define canonical naturally
labeled marked histories and cylinder probabilities from products of `q/2`.
Prove normalization, prefix consistency and existence of the classical history
measure, stating the measure-extension hypotheses. Explain finite natural-label
covariance and correct labeled multiplicities: one construction weight is not
an unlabeled terminal probability. Define a modest measurable covariant event
algebra, for example through marked stem events, without claiming completeness
for every covariant event.

Then prove or correct the proposed height screen without assuming independent
births. Sum both fair-mark branches to obtain the full-precursor probability.
For `m` births after a seed cutoff `n0`, the suggested conditional lower-tail
bound is `exp(-2 delta^2 m)` for
`H_(n0+m) <= H_n0 + (1/2-delta)m`, with `0<delta<1/2`.
Derive the corresponding almost-sure `liminf H_N/N >= 1/2`, retaining the
finite-seed offset. Strict probability greater than one half does not by
itself prove a strictly larger limiting fraction or existence of that limit.

If valid, reject this specific selection for a declared target requiring
`H_N/N -> 0`; do not call it a universal no-go for other normalizations or
all geometries. A particular Lorentzian comparison requires its actual
sampling, region and density assumptions. Height is intrinsic to each finite
parent, but an asymptotic birth-prefix density is not automatically invariant
under arbitrary infinite natural re-enumerations. Keep the finite-size regime
screen and the covariant event algebra distinct. Neither width, proper time,
posts nor physical gravity follows from the proposed bound.

Conclude with the smallest next law-selection question and its remaining
positivity/covariance obligations. Merely lowering full-ideal probabilities
is not an accepted repair. Obtain independent internal reviews and return a
source-stable handoff for root adjudication. Root continues the programme after
that boundary. The parallel measurement next step remains applicable C02
uncertainty qualification and frozen processing/CW treatment; RET stays paused.

conditional centered-Bernoulli bound and finite-history restart give the
cumulative actual-height drift criterion with distinct convergence modes.

number of tallest terminal maxima. Every parent has a preserving-only option,
but simultaneous normalization is a separate obligation. Compactness and
strict mixing prove that zero infimum worst-row drift is equivalent to
`A_0 beta=1`, `beta>=0`, for preserving-only columns.

The explicit antichain-focused selector is strictly admitted after the frozen

from size five to N with probability only `5/N`. A relation appears almost
surely and persists. Thereafter the actual drift tends to one, giving
`H(P_N)/N -> 1` almost surely and in every finite positive moment. This
rejects that exact selector; it is not a general asymptotic obstruction.
All-size balancing/propagation remains open. No numerical suite was needed
or run for this analytic adjudication. The one-note reservation is released.

The minimum number of arbitrary exceptions leaving an induced Ferrers order
is intrinsic, monotone under ideals, and increases by zero or one per maximal
birth. Every nonempty parent has a raising empty birth and at least one neutral
birth. Requiring an ideal exception set is unstable: one cloned maximum of an
N-vertex non-chain rectangle has arbitrary defect one but minimum ideal-core
cost N-2, even for balanced rectangles. Actual vertices and records stay intact.

Height/width normalized errors are at most k/N; comparable-pair density error
is at most k(2N-k-1)/(N(N-1)) when the retained order has at least two vertices.
Optimizers need not form a nested history, and sublinear defect supplies no
balance or geometric limit. For an already fixed all-size law, bounded
Bernoulli martingale differences vanish after division by N almost surely and
in L2/L1. Sublinear defect is equivalent to vanishing Cesaro conditional drift
in the corresponding mode; expectation/probability convergence alone is not
promoted to almost-sure convergence.

The finite-layer infimum of worst marked-row drift is zero exactly when the
closed component-scale row polytope admits zero drift in every row. Strict
interior mixing proves sufficiency but never attains zero for nonempty parents.
Every component with any raising occurrence must vanish on that boundary;
neutrality in one row is insufficient. The actual accepted three-vertex
mixed component retains (1/3)(1/5)=(2/3)(1/10)=1/15. All record assignments,
labeled multiplicities, full complements and passive payload remain required.
No feasible defect-control law or all-size sublinear realization is established.
